# What has been tried

Living catalogue. Check here before proposing an experiment, and update it in the same commit as any new experiment,
result, or proposal that gets dropped. Each row says what was found and why we moved on.

## Nearest prior work (not ours)

| # | What | Found | Relevance |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0a | VPD paper, future work "Interpreting parameter gradients to understand training and finetuning" (`../papers/pd/vpd_blog_post.md`, Limitations) | Proposes splitting gradients or a fine-tune weight diff over the subcomponent basis. Not run | The exact idea; nobody has executed it |
| 0b | VPD paper, "Editing a language model's parameters by hand" | Emoticon → `o` behaviour is expressed by one subcomponent (`2.mlp.down:2359`, five siblings); a hand edit of its write vector competes with a LoRA on ±20-token windows (n = 10 or 947, KL penalty λ) | Shows a one-subcomponent answer exists for this behaviour: the most favourable test case for commuting |
| 0c | VPD paper, "Geometric consistency across seeds" | Cross-seed mean max cosine similarity of rank-1 subcomponents is 0.28 (U 0.48, V 0.52); random init 0.01 | Re-decomposing from scratch does not recover the same subcomponents: any re-decomposition test needs a same-W control |
| 0d | vpd-audit (LessWrong 2026, `github.com/angsten/vpd-audit`) | Aggregating explanations across tokens drifts the model (0.34 nats own explanation → 0.80 at 64 donor tokens); deleting the 28,946 never-needed subcomponents moves the model 1.28 nats | Tests aggregation, not updates. Warns that "unimportant" subcomponents and Δ are not inert; loader pinned to `param-decomp` commit `74146b5` works on one 40 GB GPU |

## Trained / run

Codes E1–E13 are the ones the research log ("Update Decomposition", see `README.md`) uses; E12 is `experiments/new_vs_existing`, run 2026-10-20 (row E12 below).

| # | Experiment | Set-up | Found | Why we moved on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | Transport test, round 1 (`experiments/transport_test`, 2026-10-14; `reports/analyses/2026-10-14_analysis_transport-test.md`) | Released 67M Pile model + VPD decomposition, frozen Γ and Δ. Arm A: full-weight fine-tune on 365 emoticon windows (P(o) 0.999 by step 10); arm B: continued pretraining (held-out effect 0.014 nats). Splits onto fixed subcomponents: the Frobenius rescaling split on alive subcomponents, functional rescaling of all alive, k-sparse write/read edits on top-k by Frobenius contribution vs k random alive | δW is not in the span of the rank-1 subcomponents: Frobenius fraction 0.05% (arm B, scored: predicted ≥ 10× random, got 1.5×; reversed) and 0.06% (arm A, reported) vs 0.035% for a random direction. Functionally the emoticon behaviour is reproduced by 10 edited subcomponents, but 10 *random* ones do as well (0.987 vs 1.000; predicted margin ≥ 0.3; reversed); at k = 3 top beats random by 0.16. The paper's `2359` ranks 2nd in its matrix at step 10 and disperses by step 30. Decomposition stays faithful under every transport by the stochastic metric (ratios 0.94–1.02); adversarial excursion to 2.4 at one checkpoint, one block. Arm B functional fits failed to optimise (training KL rose; lr 1e-2 too large), so that half is uninformative; arm A's span fraction relative to random grows to 10× by step 300 (review `reports/reviews/2026-10-14_review_round1-audit.md`) | The null (free write vectors) is too expressive and the Frobenius selector picks the wrong subcomponents; next round needs a scale-only or constrained null, a functional selector, checkpoints at steps 1–5, a larger arm-B effect, and selectivity on generic text. Through `/design` first. Also, measured against the user's precise question (`GOALS.md`, 2026-10-14), round 1's faithfulness check let Δ absorb δW, so it cannot tell a good map Φ from the trivial "do nothing" one |
| E2 | Least-squares split test, round 2 (`experiments/lift_test`, 2026-10-14; `reports/analyses/2026-10-14_analysis_lift-test.md`, `notebooks/E02_lift_test.ipynb`) | Released 67M model + decomposition, Γ frozen, emoticon arm A. Obstruction ω of full-weight (Adam, SGD) and LoRA updates, Frobenius and input-weighted; behaviour of the expressible part δW − O(δW); **outside**: least-squares split of a finished fine-tune; **inside**: component training (train alive U, V, Δ fixed) | Inside works: component training learns the behaviour (P(o) 0.95 by step 3) with a third of the fine-tune's off-target KL and stays faithful on every metric (PGD ratio 1.03 at step 10, 1.07 at 30; P3′ passed). Outside fails: the least-squares split carries the update (88–93% of the unmasked gap) but is adversarially fragile (PGD 1.14 → 5.41 over steps 1–10; P2′ reversed). The outside the subcomponents part of a fine-tune is ~30% input-weighted (W₀: 13%; Frobenius measure useless, W₀ fails it too), Adam or SGD alike (SGD at 0.1 to 1 only, all with large off-target damage; a cleaner SGD rate is untried); the expressible part alone reproduces the behaviour, but the outside the subcomponents part alone also partly carries it (redundant, not unneeded; review `reports/reviews/2026-10-15_review_obstruction-claim.md`). P1 reversed; first P2/P3 untestable as filed (blind weight term; lr grid too large) | One task, the easiest (an existing behaviour), 30 steps. The open questions are whether inside training survives a behaviour the model lacks (the "new capabilities" branch) and longer training (the limit); cause of the split's fragility unknown. **Re-scoped 2026-10-15** (`reports/reviews/2026-10-15_review_faithfulness-scope.md`): every faithfulness ratio was computed on Pile rows holding ~3 trigger positions in 131k, so "faithful" means the decomposition's account of the *old* model and the fine-tune's side-effects survived; whether the trained behaviour is carried by Γ-gated components is unmeasured for both routes. The component training was never run on a LoRA, and its (U, V) states were not saved. Round 3 waits on the task-local harness |
| E3 | Identity test (`experiments/identity_test`, 2026-10-15; `reports/analyses/2026-10-15_analysis_identity-test.md`) | Round 2's component training (steps 10, 30) and least-squares split (W steps 2, 3, 10), regenerated. Revert-one attribution of the held-out log-odds gain over all 9 881 alive subcomponents; gauge-free identity (x·v profile retrieval, ablation-effect cosine vs split-half floor) of the top 20 against a same-matrix null | Identity kept 20/20 but trivially: changes are 0.2–1.3% of u vᵀ and the null group scores the same; no positive control, so uninformative. The update is extremely diffuse: top 10 remove 1.6% of the gain (component training), top 1 000 36%; least-squares split ~2.5× more concentrated. `2359` not strengthened (scale 1.000, drop ≤ 0.008) | The component training is faithful but its update is not attributable to a few subcomponents, nor to the one the paper labels; open whether that is Adam or the task. Partly answered by the E4 follow-up: SGD's top 10 remove 0.16 of the gain, still not concentrated, measured at a smaller size of change than Adam's; a matched comparison is running (item 2 of `reports/reviews/2026-10-21_review_sgd-finding-influence.md`) |
| E4 | Concentration test (`experiments/concentration_test`, 2026-10-15; `reports/analyses/2026-10-15_analysis_concentration-test.md`) | Component-training arms: Adam all, SGD all, Adam on top 1/10/100 by gauge-free |dL/ds|, Adam on `2359` + 5 siblings only. Pile faithfulness with 3-seed W₀ floor; trigger-local CI-masked P(o) on held-out windows with do-nothing control; identity with swap/random/rotation controls | Top 1, top 10 and siblings-only all learn (KL elsewhere 0.011–0.020), keep Pile ratios ≤ 1.08 and give CI-masked P(o) 0.98 at the trigger; Adam-all only 0.64, do-nothing 0.53 (control missed its rule by 0.037, so trigger part unscored). Γ's masks alone carry much of the behaviour (unexplained). SGD found no lr within the off-target limit in 0.3–30 (P1 unscorable); corrected by the E4 follow-up row (0.003–0.1 learn; P1 fail, as predicted). Siblings change ~100% in u vᵀ, write direction turns ~45° | Amendment 1 (Γ's gates fixed to W₀, control with power): no arm's components carry it under W₀'s mask pattern (siblings 0.37, top 10 0.10, Adam-all 0.006 × target; A1, A2 reversed). The sparse arms work only through Γ re-gating on the new activations, and the moved subcomponents are already gated on at W₀ (g 0.83–0.93). Amendment 2: every update (Adam-all, top 1, top 10, siblings) switches on largely the same ~150–200 pre-existing subcomponents at the trigger (off at W₀; Jaccard 0.59–0.88). They carry about half the re-gating effect (0.45–0.64; random set 0.002) and each pushes weakly towards `o` (collective, 5 SE over null). B1 narrowly failed, B2 and B3 passed. Evidence for "reveal" via Γ's gating |
| E5 | Task ladder, round 3 (`experiments/task_ladder`, 2026-10-19; `reports/analyses/2026-10-19_analysis_task-ladder.md`) | 6 mined trigger → target tasks at rungs 0–2 (baseline P 0.25 to 0.003), two per rung at high/low Γ coverage, plus emoticon. Arms: do-nothing, Adam component training, gated-off component training; route split at the trigger (d, r, i, e_gate, e_Δ) | Hypothesis refuted. Changed subcomponents carry 0.03–0.08 of the gain; the released subcomponents masked with the trained model's CI values carry most of it, about equally for full fine-tune, all-subcomponent component training and low-CI-only component training. P1 (coverage) null (filed pass; rung 0 missed m by 0.007), P2 null at rung 2 (filed pass), A1 null and A2 pass as filed. r orders by rung (0.75–0.92 at rungs 0–1, 0.3–0.6 at rung 2; the two rung-2 tasks differ by 0.32 at matched baseline), not by coverage; at rung 2 e_gate dominates. G1b (high-end control) did not learn | Coverage has nothing to predict while the direct share is ≤ 8%. The ladder's retained_pgd_all / retained_pgd_trigger have no power (`reports/reviews/2026-10-19_review_pgd-local.md`: the shared adversary saturates on alike windows, seed spread 55–88 nats against 4–15 nat gains) and are reported-only. Next: decide whether the re-masking share r is a mechanism or a leak through Γ's input (user, via /design) |
| E6 | Recruit test (`experiments/recruit_test`, 2026-10-19; `reports/analyses/2026-10-19_analysis_recruit-test.md`, `notebooks/E06_recruit_test.ipynb`) | Do-nothing placement (δW in Δ, released components unchanged, Γ frozen) of the REAL updates on the emoticon task: W fine-tune step 10 (2 seeds), LoRA r8 on `h.2.mlp.down_proj` step 10, two fine-tunes to a different target token at the same trigger (the null with power), a Frobenius-matched random δW, and component training's δW as reference (amendment 2 reproduced exactly). Route split, switched-on set S_on (Δg > 0.3 at the trigger), Jaccard, recovery | The real fine-tune recruits component training's set (J 0.64; seed floor 0.93) and the LoRA's (0.82); a different-target fine-tune of the same size recruits a disjoint set (J 0.04–0.05). Re-gating the W₀ components alone gives r 0.60 (fine-tune), 0.54 (LoRA), and the same 0.55–0.60 for the nulls: r is not target-specific, the set is. S_on carries only 0.40 of the re-gating effect (P4 failed; random set 0.01): the overlaps compare which gates moved, not what produces the behaviour. Rank-r component training on the LoRA's matrix still never run at this point (this LoRA arm is a normal LoRA placed do-nothing). Random δW inert (P5). R1/R2 (is the set the model's `o` machinery at W₀?) not run: 96 natural-`o` positions in 256 rows, 200 needed. Surprises: Γ is a global shared transformer over all 24 matrices' inputs and all positions, so the LoRA-locality gate G5 rested on a wrong premise (layer-2 edit moves layer-0 gates); null_a fell back to `<|endoftext|>` through an all-inf argmin (rule since replaced) | Reveal through routing is a property of the real update and the target, not of our map. Open: what the recruited set did at W₀ (needs more Pile rows), the switched-off set, tasks the model lacks (ladder) |
| E7 | LoRA commute (`experiments/lora_commute`, 2026-10-19; `reports/analyses/2026-10-19_analysis_lora-commute.md`, `notebooks/E07_lora_commute.ipynb`) | LoRA ranks 1, 4, 8, 32 on `h.2.mlp.down_proj` and the full-weight fine-tune, each measured do-nothing and least-squares split; component training confined to that matrix (rank-r LoRA-shaped offsets, free offsets) and on all 24; route split, outside share, round 2's Pile harness with a 3-seed W₀ floor, W₀ task-local seeds | The user's instinct (a LoRA is easier to decompose) failed on every number. Outside share of the LoRA at the isotropic null at every rank (0.38–0.43 vs 0.42; the read factor stays near its random init), full fine-tune 0.27 vs 0.53; the LoRA's least-squares split leaves e_Δ 0.33–0.43 outside the subcomponents (fine-tune's split 0.085); Pile PGD ratios (W₀ + 3 floors = 1.11; filed breaks line 1.5): LoRA splits 1.35–2.0 (only rank 8 over 1.5), LoRA do-nothing 1.39, fine-tune's split 7.3 (round 2: 5.4; G4 missed by 35%, cause not established), one-matrix component-training runs 2.1–4.1 (rank 8 worst), pb_full 1.39, pb_all 1.10. d 0.05–0.11 and r 0.5–0.6 for every arm. P1, P2, P5, C2 null; P3, P4, C1, C3 neither | Rank is not the axis. **Corrected 2026-10-19** (`reports/reviews/2026-10-19_review_lora-failure-and-fixes.md`): the site is the confound, not the LoRA. The decomposition reads 21% of the paper's matrix's input energy, so even the fine-tune's own update there is 0.36 outside the subcomponents (isotropic null 0.42; the LoRA 0.41), while the fine-tune's energy sits on matrices the decomposition reads fully (four saturated matrices carry 38% of it). Round 4 compared a LoRA on a badly-read matrix with a fine-tune on well-read ones. Fix: the matched-matrix LoRA (`experiments/lora_matched`) |
| E8 | Matched-matrix LoRA, round 5 (`experiments/lora_matched`, 2026-10-19; `reports/analyses/2026-10-19_analysis_lora-matched.md`) | Rank-8 LoRA on all 24 matrices (the standard placement) and the full-weight fine-tune, both do-nothing and least-squares split through the shared harness (`src/commute/harness.py`): route split, outside share per matrix and pooled, Pile ratios with a 3-seed W₀ floor; the fine-tune's least-squares split rerun on an L40 as the control | The site was round 4's confound. The LoRA's read side stays at its random init (M1 passed: pooled outside share 0.86 of the isotropic null, \|cos\| 1.000) but 60% of its energy lands on the four saturated matrices, so its least-squares split leaves e_Δ 0.22 (one-site LoRA 0.33–0.43, ft 0.09; M2 neither) and the split state is at the W₀ floor under the Pile adversary (PGD 1.13; fine-tune's split 5.41 in the same job; C2m neither by the letter, reversed in direction). d flat (C1m pass). G4 closed: the L40 reproduces round 2's 2.18 / 5.41 to three digits, so round 4's 7.31 was the 4090. W₀ PGD floor 21% on the L40 (4090: 3.5%) | The LoRA learned at step 2, where the fine-tune's least-squares split was also at the floor (round 2, step 1): the step-matched Pile comparison (LoRA least-squares split at steps 10 and 100) is not run and decides whether a LoRA's least-squares split survives where the fine-tune's does not |
| E9 | LoRA to its plateau (`experiments/lora_plateau`, 2026-10-19; `reports/analyses/2026-10-19_analysis_lora-plateau.md`, `notebooks/E09_lora_plateau.ipynb`) | Round 5's rank-8 LoRA (all 24 matrices, lr 1e-3, seed 0) to 1000 steps; at 9 checkpoints the pooled outside share ÷ its isotropic null, A's overlap with the alive read span, \|cos\| with its init; do-nothing and least-squares split with the Pile harness at steps 10, 100, 1000 (L40S) | A moved 10% and the ratio stayed 0.82–1.02 at every checkpoint (P1 pass): the read side never becomes readable; the energy moved from the saturated `h.3.mlp.c_fc` (60%) to `h.3.attn.q_proj`/`k_proj` (68%, nulls 0.84/0.77), so 62% of the update over all 24 matrices is outside the subcomponents at the plateau. The split state sits at the W₀ floor under the Pile adversary at every step (1.26, 1.22, 1.07; do-nothing 1.17, 1.09, 1.04), including the fine-tune's matched step 10 where the fine-tune's least-squares split read 5.41 (P2 pass; round 4's C2 reopened on the L40 reference, no fine-tune arm on this card). At the plateau e_Δ rose to 0.31 and Δ took 0.54 of ‖δW‖² (P3 neither: Pile half as predicted, Δ half missed). W₀ PGD floor 4.6% on the L40S against 21% on the L40 | "Let A become data-shaped by training longer" (the lora_matched notebook's candidate 3) is closed; the open items are the fine-tune control on the same card and the plateau's e_Δ seed spread |
| E10 | Key → value task, round 6 (`experiments/kv_task`, 2026-10-19; `reports/analyses/2026-10-19_analysis_key-value-task.md`) | A behaviour the model lacks: after a zero-frequency marker, a common word is followed by a fixed random other word (word-level substitution cipher; K = 1 and K = 32 pairs, baseline P < 1e-4) or by itself (identity control, same format, baseline ≤ 0.002). Per task: W fine-tune (do-nothing placement), its least-squares split (metric control), Adam component training on all alive (U, V), 300 steps, scored at the first checkpoint with held-out P ≥ 0.5; task-local harness, route split, round 2's Pile ratios, VPD's sparsity loss (new) | Everything learned (fine-tune at step 10; component training at 10 for K = 1, 30 for K = 32). Pile PGD ratio of component training 1.20 (rand1), 1.19 (ident32), **2.81 (rand32; filed pass, reversed)** against least-squares splits 1.73 / 9.4 / 38.9 and do-nothing 1.06 / 8.1 / 4.7. The identity is carried by Γ re-switching existing subcomponents (r 0.76–0.80), the random maps by subcomponents Γ does not switch on (e_gate 0.75–0.86): GOALS's reveal vs create, read off the data; retained_ci_masked 0.77 vs 0.24, the same for component training and do-nothing (−0.03). Sparsity loss is identical for do-nothing and least-squares split on every task: with Γ frozen it depends on W′ only and cannot tell maps apart; its filed rule (P4) also mis-set its floor, FAIL ×3 as filed | The weak version holds for one new association and the identity, breaks on Pile for 32 at ‖δW‖/‖W₀‖ 0.015; cause not separated (update size, off-target damage, K, scored step). Next: Pile harness on the saved ident32 states at matched size (steps 100/300) and rand32 at 100/300; then K between 1 and 32 |

| E12 | New vs existing signatures, round 7 (`experiments/new_vs_existing`, 2026-10-20; `reports/analyses/2026-10-20_analysis_new-vs-existing-signatures.md`, `notebooks/E12_new_vs_existing.ipynb`) | Two copy tasks (M x → x) and two random-mapping tasks (M x → π(x), K = 32) in the key → value format, each a full fine-tune at 2 seeds, scored at the first learned checkpoint; per state: outside share ÷ null, e_Δ of the least-squares split, relevance at W₀ of the 100 subcomponents the least-squares split moves most (T) vs CI-matched random sets, route split, seed Jaccard of T; reported: T's CI at the trigger, least-squares split energy on switched-off subcomponents, Pile on-rate, switched-on sets, coverage | Only the route separates (r 0.76–0.80 vs 0.27–0.29, e_gate 0.03–0.11 vs 0.45–0.53; P4 pass, reproduces round 6 to three digits). T is busy rather than specific: mean CI 0.18 at the trigger, 95% of least-squares split energy on switched-off subcomponents, on at 13% of Pile positions (alive mean 1.5%), ablation moves the trigger distribution 10–12 nats (random 0.5) in both classes; T ∩ switched-on ≤ 0.03. ## Proposed and dropped

| What | Dropped | Why |
|---|---|---|
| LoRA appended to the decomposition as new rank-1 subcomponent(s) with their own gate (definition (iii) of the 2026-10-15 "LoRA through component training" entry, now rank-r component training) | 2026-10-15, user, at `/design` | It assumes that every update adds a new capability (the thing under test) and cannot say whether existing subcomponents explain the update. Its gate is either a trained net (breaks "simple") or always-on (Δ absorbing δW). Arms (i) same-matrix and (ii) same-rank component training were kept (`backlog.md`) |
| New-skill test as planned on 2026-10-14 (`reports/plans/2026-10-14_plan_new-skill-test.md`: one synthetic trigger → target task the model lacks, falsifier = CI-masked ratio on Pile) | 2026-10-15, user ("not ready") | Its falsifier had no power: Pile rows hold ~3 trigger positions in 131k (`reports/reviews/2026-10-15_review_faithfulness-scope.md`). Superseded by the task ladder (`reports/plans/2026-10-15_plan_task-ladder.md`), where "a behaviour the model lacks" is rung 4 of a graded ladder measured with the task-local harness |
| Read-side LoRA (A initialised in, or trained first towards, the span of the site's alive read vectors; the "obvious next arm" of round 4) | 2026-10-19, user, at `/design` (`reports/reviews/2026-10-19_review_lora-failure-and-fixes.md`, 3.1) | With A frozen in span(V_A) it is the write-only half of `pb_r`'s space, which round 4 measured already: exactly expressible (e_Δ 0.004–0.011) and still fragile (Pile PGD 2.1–4.1). With A trainable the only new quantity is how fast it drifts, a one-minute trajectory, not an arm. Departs from decision 16 (nobody ships a LoRA initialised from a decomposition). Patch, not a step |
| Toy ground-truth testbed for strengthen vs create (ResidualMLP, then a from-scratch transformer on a task mixture; proposed in chat 2026-10-15 after `/design`) | 2026-10-15, user | ResidualMLP's "create" is a disjoint new feature, not a composed skill; a toy transformer needs a from-scratch VPD run (hours, unvalidated). The user preferred smaller, cheaper experiments on the 67M model first |

| E127 (ls_start_faith) | **Ran (MATS debug L40, $0, every control exact unless noted; provisional, no independent check). Main run jobs 54836 (stopped at its budget, exit 3, as planned) and 54874, ~31 min. Amendment 1 job 54890, ~19 min, stopped with exit 1 at its 9th of 12 reads on the emoticon standard copy's free control (1.0070 vs stored 0.9866, band 1e-2; most likely bf16 rounding, not proven); lowercase complete. Amendment 2 (the last) job 54934, 15.5 min, exit 0. Report `reports/analyses/2026-11-06_analysis_e127-ls-start-faithfulness.md` (incl. "Amendment 1", "Amendment 2"), notebook `notebooks/E127_ls_start_faith.ipynb`, figures `experiments/ls_start_faith/figures/` (`amend1_hold.png`, `amend2_delta.png`), plan `reports/plans/2026-11-06_plan_e127-ls-start-faithfulness.md`.** Why the least-squares start reads worse than do-nothing under E34's adversarial check: the start's own weight change S in Δ ("same function in Δ") splits the excess. Lowercase ratios: do-nothing 4.79, same function in Δ 2.95, start 8.33; emoticon 1.39, 1.15, 1.57. Source **placement** on both, function part negative (emoticon one seed, weak). Hold (alive masks at 1) removes it, but Amendment 1: do-nothing held 1.194 lowercase against teeth bar 2.90, 0.877 emoticon: **no teeth**, exposure reading **unreadable under the hold**. Random masks "adversary only"; cancellation "does not track". **Amendment 2 reversed the filed lean** (Fable: standard copy's change in Δ 1.24, band 1.10-1.45): each copy's change S_c = U'V'ᵀ − UVᵀ read on the subcomponents / in Δ: standard copy 1.913 / **3.526** (R1 "function fails too", lean 0.25), copy from the start 9.405 / 3.961, rank-8 LoRA copy 1.326 / 2.336; excess multiplier m 0.36 / 2.84 / 0.24 against the start's 3.77. Teacher training's loss (`src/commute/teacher.py:40-47`, via `row_copy.py:81-82`, `component_training.py:68-74,152-158`) is plain KL at full masks: no mask, CI, stochastic or adversarial term. | Placement cuts both ways: the least-squares split's multiplies the excess ~4x, teacher training's divides it ~3-4x (by accident of its optimisation, not by design). E127's 3.8x is a property of the least-squares placement, not of placement in general. The broad lowercase failure is not a good function exposed by placement: every copy's change fails in Δ (2.34-3.96 vs do-nothing 4.79; standard copy keeps 67% of do-nothing's excess), and teacher training's placement hides most of it, enough for LoRA (1.33 < 1.61), not for full-matrix Adam (1.91). "Fragile" is under the Δ-placed check, whose adversary freedom differs. Copy's distance from δW reads as placement-shaping, not dropped collateral. E127 closed (two amendments used); next as new plans: where each change sits against CI on the check's rows; teacher training with a faithfulness term. |
